&lt;project_context>
# 项目: trace-verdict-study

## 运行环境
- 服务器名: westd-11235
- SSH 账户: root（调用 ssh_execute / submit_training_job 时传入 account="root"）
- SSH 连接命令: ssh connect.westd.seetacloud.com  # 必须用此命令，~/.ssh/config 自动应用密钥和跳板机，禁止用裸 IP
- 项目代码根目录: /root/trace-verdict-study


## 环境搭建
连接服务器后先调 `detect_image` 检测是否为预置镜像。如果匹配，跳过基础环境搭建，只装项目特有依赖。已预装的包不要重复安装。

## 输出风格
回复简洁直接。只输出执行结果和必要信息，不要解释过程、不要客套、不要给未被要求的建议。
&lt;/project_context>
你是 ML 学术论文写作助手。撰写 EMNLP 2026 Main (ARR) 论文的 Section 3: Experimental Setup。

## 投稿信息
- Venue: EMNLP 2026 Main (ARR May 25)
- Format: 8 pages + unlimited refs + appendix
- Paper type: Empirical study
- Title direction: "Exception Is All You Need: Fault Detection as a Universal Mechanism for Verdict Prediction"

## Story Arc
1. Setup: Trace SFT for code tasks — promising but inconsistent (DCSH, TracePile). Unknown whether traces help verdict tasks.
2. First result: no_trace 75.1% → full_trace 81.8% → VOTA 99.41%. Label-only loss ablation: 26% gradient dilution, 74% information overload.
3. Twist: Component ablation reveals exception_only (99.83%) ≥ VOTA (99.41%). Verdict prediction is fault detection, not trace reasoning.
4. Cross-model stress test: exception transfers with 0.50pp gap; VOTA degrades 14.96pp on DeepSeek.
5. Design principle: Extract highest-specificity diagnostic signal; discard everything else.

## 你的章节: Section 3 — Experimental Setup (~1.5 pages)

### 3.1 Task Definition
- Verdict prediction: given (code, test_input, expected_output, [trace]), predict pass/fail
- Binary classification via SFT (instruction-tuned format, output is single token "pass" or "fail")
- 不同于 code generation — 输出空间是 {pass, fail}

### 3.2 Dataset
- CRUXEval (Gu et al., ICML 2024) — 800 Python functions
- 我们的 split: 4720 train / 1190 test
- 数据集来源: Wang et al. (EMNLP Findings 2025, 即 DCSH) 构建的 verdict 版本
- Trace 生成: Python tracer 提取 full execution trace
- Pass/fail 比例: majority baseline = 71.51% (pass)

### 3.3 VOTA Trace Representation
Variable-Outcome Trace Abstraction 的三个组件:
1. **Exception summary**: `[ERROR] type: ... message: ...` — 结构化错误事件
2. **Assertion-point (actual/expected)**: `[ASSERT] expected: X actual: Y` — 关键检查点值快照
3. **Loop compression**: 循环行为模式摘要 — 迭代计数和变量变化趋势
- 对比 full trace: VOTA 是 targeted extraction 而非 lossy compression
- 需要配 example (可用 Figure placeholder)

### 3.4 Experimental Conditions
**7 conditions on Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct:**
| Condition | Description |
|-----------|-------------|
| no_trace | Code + test I/O only, no execution trace |
| full_trace | Complete execution trace (truncated to 2048 tokens with label preservation) |
| VOTA | Full VOTA representation (exception + ae + loop) |
| exception_only | Only [ERROR] events from VOTA |
| ae_only | Only actual/expected assertion values from VOTA |
| loop_only | Only loop compression summaries from VOTA |
| label_only_loss | Full trace input but loss computed only on verdict token |

**4 conditions on DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B-Instruct:**
no_trace, full_trace, VOTA, exception_only

**Training details (所有条件统一):**
- LoRA: rank=16, alpha=32, target_modules=all linear
- Epochs: 3
- Learning rate: 2e-4
- Per-device batch size: 4, gradient accumulation: 8 (effective batch: 32)
- Max sequence length: 2048 tokens
- Gradient checkpointing: enabled
- Hardware: 2× NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 (96GB each), CUDA 12.8
- Seeds: primary seed=42; robustness seeds 123, 456 for exception_only
- Evaluation: accuracy, per-class precision/recall/F1, ECE, Brier score

## Notation Table (全文统一)
- $\mathbf{x} = (c, i, o_{\text{exp}})$: code snippet $c$, test input $i$, expected output $o_{\text{exp}}$
- $t$: full execution trace
- $\text{VOTA}(t)$: Variable-Outcome Trace Abstraction
- $\hat{y} \in \{\text{pass}, \text{fail}\}$: predicted verdict

## 写作规范
- 英文，LaTeX 格式
- EMNLP: Limitations section 是强制要求（本 section 不需要写）
- 引用用 \cite{placeholder} 占位
- 数字必须来自实际实验结果
- 禁止 AI 痕迹句式（"In this paper, we propose...", "Extensive experiments demonstrate..."）
- Figure/Table 用 placeholder（\begin{figure/table}...caption...\end{figure/table}）

1. `read_registry(project="trace-verdict-study")` 获取训练细节
2. `read_project_file(project="trace-verdict-study", file="memory.md")` 获取环境信息和决策

Wang et al. (EMNLP 2025): trace SFT 效果因模型而异，\\\"no single trace representation consistently outperforms others\\\"\\n2. Ferrando et al. (2025): compact scratchpad ≥ full scratchpad\\n3. Gao et al. (2025): \\\"base model inductive bias outweighs SFT corpus\\\"\\n4. Meta CWM (2025): execution trace mid-training 需要 base model \\\"trace readiness\\\"\\n\\n### Discussion 假说（论文 Discussion 中应呈现为 hypothesis，列出 alternative explanations）\\n- H1: Execution reasoning readiness（CRUXEval gap 预测 VOTA gap）— 最有文献支撑\\n- H2: Pretraining scale × structured data（Qwen 5.5T vs DeepSeek 2T tokens, 2.75x）— 合理但不能作为 definitive conclusion\\n- H3: Base model inductive bias ceiling（train loss 相当但泛化差距大）\\n\\n#### Alternative explanations（论文 Discussion 必须列出）\\n- Tokenizer 效率差异（不同 tokenizer 对 trace 文本的编码效率可能不同）\\n- Chat template 差异（不同模型的 chat template 对 trace 格式的适配程度不同）\\n- Instruction tuning 数据质量差异（RLHF/DPO 阶段的对齐方式可能影响 SFT 下游任务）\\n- ⚠️ 数据修正：DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B 预训练 **2T tokens**（87% code + 13% NL），来源 DeepSeek-Coder 技术报告。cross-model-trace-lit Worker 产出的 \\\"1.17T\\\" 和 \\\"4.7x\\\" 为错误数据，不得引用\\n\\n#### ⭐ 新视角：exception signal 免疫于 trace utilization gap [2026-05-15]\\n- VOTA 跨模型 gap 14.96pp → exception_only gap 仅 0.50pp\\n- 解释：exception events 结构简单（[ERROR] type: ... message: ...），不需要深度 trace reasoning 能力\\n- CRUXEval 差距只影响需要理解复杂 trace 结构（loop patterns, variable flow）的组件\\n- **论文可 claim**: exception-based fault detection is model-agnostic; trace complexity tolerance varies by model capability\\n\\n## 环境信息 [2026-05-14]\\n- westd-11235: 2x RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell 94GB, CUDA 12.8\\n- 模型通过 ModelScope CDN 下载\\n- \\\"pass\\\"/\\\"fail\\\" 在 Qwen/DeepSeek tokenizer 中均为单 token\\n- Qwen2.5-Coder chat template overhead: 31 tokens\\n\\n## 流程教训\\n- submit_training_job 必须用，不能用 nohup\\n- Worker 汇报需要交叉验证（exit code + log）\\n- [2026-05-15] 数据格式化 token budget 必须扣除 chat template overhead\\n- [2026-05-15] 所有训练必须加 --gradient_checkpointing\\n- [2026-05-15] HuggingFace 下载设 HF_HUB_DISABLE_XET=1，优先 ModelScope CDN\\n- [2026-05-15] Worker 长时间无产出必须主动 get_worker_status(verbose=True) 排查\\n- [2026-05-15] bash -c 内部必须显式 export CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES={N}\\n- [2026-05-15] GPU 碰撞：提交前 nvidia-smi 确认目标 GPU 无其他进程\\n- [2026-05-15] **同 GPU 并行 eval+training 严重降速**：cuda:1 上 eval+training 并行导致训练从 7s/step 恶化到 35-49s/step。eval 必须用独立 GPU 或等训练完成。不要因\\\"eval 几分钟就完\\\"而在同一 GPU 上并行\\n- [2026-05-15] **重复 Worker 竞态**：两个同名 Worker 相隔 30 秒启动，后者看到前者的修改误判为\\\"原有\\\"。派 Worker 前查 list_workers 确认无重复任务\\n- [2026-05-15] **Bootstrap 方法一致性**：同一论文表格中所有 pairwise CI 必须用同一方法（paired 或 unpaired）。evaluate.py 必须加 --save_predictions 保存 per-sample 结果\\n\\n## 文献定位 [2026-05-15]\\n- Wang et al. (EMNLP 2025): 原始 trace 对 SFT 帮助有限\\n- APPT (TSE 2024): 最近 competitor，BERT 非 decoder，无 trace\\n- **文献空白**：无现有工作做 compressed execution trace + SFT for binary verdict\\n- Ferrando et al. 2025: compact ≥ full scratchpad\\n- TraceCoder (ICSE 2026): 选择性 probe 优于全量 trace\\n- TraceRepair (2026): critical variable snapshots > 全量\\n- Demystifying Errors (2025): LLM 值追踪能力弱\\n\", \"total_chars\": 9547, \"truncated\": false}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {}}"}

|\\n\\n#### DeepSeek full_trace 坍塌 [2026-05-15]\\n- 28.5% accuracy, 100% 预测 fail (pass_recall=0, fail_recall=1)\\n- 同 Qwen v2 full_trace 坍塌模式 — chat template overhead 导致 label 截断\\n- 诊断中：所有条件共享用 Qwen tokenizer overhead=32 格式化的数据，DeepSeek chat template overhead 不同\\n- **不影响其他 3 条件**：no_trace/VOTA/exception_only 输入远短于 full_trace，不触发截断\\n- DeepSeek epoch-level: eval_loss epoch2→3 从 0.090 升至 [2026-05-15]\\n- **exception_only 跨模型 gap 仅 0.50pp**（99.83% vs 99.33%），而 VOTA gap 14.96pp\\n- VOTA 退化到 84.45% 说明 loop/ae compression 对不同 tokenizer/architecture 不 robust\\n- 但 exception signal extraction 本身是 **model-agnostic** 的\\n- **论文 headline 应围绕 exception signal，不是全 VOTA 压缩**\\n- Director 决策：exception_only seed robustness 优先于 VOTA seed robustness\\n- DeepSeek exception_only epoch1 = **Pass 偏向严重**: VOTA fail recall 仅 45.4%\\n- **DeepSeek exception_only: 99.33%** — within-model lift +25.97pp over no_trace\\n\\n#### ⚠️ Bootstrap 方法一致性待修（非紧急）[2026-05-15]\\n- Qwen 实验用 **paired** bootstrap（有 per-sample predictions）\\n- DeepSeek 当前用 **unpaired** bootstrap（缺 per-sample predictions）→ CI 更宽，是保守估计\\n- **论文同一张表不能混用两种方法**\\n- **待办**: 为 deepseek_vota_seed42 和 deepseek_no_trace_seed42 重跑 `evaluate.py --save_predictions`，再用 paired bootstrap 重算 CI\\n- 当前 unpaired 结果 (+11.09pp [+7.90, +14.37]) 方向正确，paired CI 只会更窄\\n\\n#### eval_loss 与 verdict accuracy 解耦（关键发现）[2026-05-15]\\n- Epoch 1: eval_loss 0.068, accuracy **78.15%**, fail_recall **23.3%**\\n- Epoch 3: eval_loss 0.112, accuracy **84.45%**, fail_recall **45.4%**\\n- eval_loss 上升 (+65%) 但 accuracy 提升 (+6.3pp), fail_recall 大幅提升 (+22.1pp)\\n- **结论**：eval_loss（token-level calibration）与 binary classification accuracy 解耦。eval_loss 不是 verdict task 的可靠 checkpoint selection criterion\\n- **论文价值**: Discussion 中应讨论此现象\\n\\n### `load_best_model_at_end` 代码版本时间线 [2026-05-15]\\n- **13:24 之前**的所有实验：使用 epoch 3 final checkpoint（包括全部 Qwen + deepseek_vota/no_trace/exception_only）\\n- **13:24 Worker 新增** full_trace 28.4% 根因是 chat template overhead bug，v5 修复后 81.8%\\n\\n### 决策教训 [2026-05-15]\\n- 判断 data leakage 必须看原始数据内容，不能仅凭比率+accuracy 异常推断\\n\\n## DeepSeek vs Qwen trace utilization gap — 文献解释（HYPOTHESIS，非 definitive）[2026-05-15]\\n\\n### CRUXEval 执行推理能力差距\\n| Model | CRUXEval-I (CoT) | CRUXEval-O (CoT) |\\n|-------|-------------------|-------------------|\\n| Qwen2.5-Coder-7B | 56.5% | 56.0% |\\n| DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B | 39.0% | 41.0% |\\n| **差距** | **+17.5pp** | **+15.0pp** |\\n\\nCRUXEval 15-17pp 差距与 VOTA accuracy 14.75pp 差距吻合。\\n\\n### 关键文献\\n1. Wang et al. (EMNLP 2025): trace SFT 效果因模型而异，\\\"no single trace representation consistently outperforms others\\\"\\n2. Ferrando et al. (2025): compact scratchpad ≥ full scratchpad\\n3. Gao et al. (2025): \\\"base model inductive bias outweighs SFT corpus\\\"\\n4. Meta CWM (2025): execution trace mid-training 需要 base model \\\"trace readiness\\\"\\n\\n### Discussion 假说（论文 Discussion 中应呈现为 hypothesis，列出 alternative explanations）\\n- H1: Execution reasoning readiness（CRUXEval gap 预测 VOTA gap）— 最有文献支撑\\n- H2: Pretraining scale × structured data（Qwen 5.5T vs DeepSeek 2T tokens, 2.75x）— 合理但不能作为 definitive conclusion\\n- H3: Base model inductive bias ceiling（train loss 相当但泛化差距大）\\n\\n#### Alternative explanations（论文 Discussion 必须列出）\\n- Tokenizer 效率差异（不同 tokenizer 对 trace 文本的编码效率可能不同）\\n- Chat template 差异（不同模型的 chat template 对 trace 格式的适配程度不同）\\n- Instruction tuning 数据质量差异（RLHF/DPO 阶段的对齐方式可能影响 SFT 下游任务）\\n- ⚠️ 数据修正：DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B 预训练 **2T tokens**（87% code + 13% NL），来源 DeepSeek-Coder 技术报告。cross-model-trace-lit Worker 产出的 \\\"1.17T\\\" 和 \\\"4.7x\\\" 为错误数据，不得引用\\n\\n#### ⭐ 新视角：exception signal 免疫于 trace utilization gap [2026-05-15]\\n- VOTA 跨模型 gap 14.96pp → exception_only gap 仅 0.50pp\\n- 解释：exception events 结构简单（[ERROR] type: ... message: ...），不需要深度 trace reasoning 能力\\n- CRUXEval 差距只影响需要理解复杂 trace 结构（loop patterns, variable flow）的组件\\n- **论文可 claim**: exception-based fault detection is model-agnostic; trace complexity tolerance varies by model capability\\n\\n## 环境信息 [2026-05-14]\\n- westd-11235: 2x RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell 94GB, CUDA 12.8\\n- 模型通过 ModelScope CDN 下载\\n- \\\"pass\\\"/\\\"fail\\\" 在 Qwen/DeepSeek tokenizer 中均为单 token\\n- Qwen2.5-Coder chat template overhead: 31 tokens\\n\\n## 流程教训\\n- submit_training_job 必须用，不能用 nohup\\n- Worker 汇报需要交叉验证（exit code + log）\\n- [2026-05-15] 数据格式化 token budget 必须扣除 chat template overhead\\n- [2026-05-15] 所有训练必须加 --gradient_checkpointing\\n- [2026-05-15] HuggingFace 下载设 HF_HUB_DISABLE_XET=1，优先 ModelScope CDN\\n- [2026-05-15] Worker 长时间无产出必须主动 get_worker_status(verbose=True) 排查\\n- [2026-05-15] bash -c 内部必须显式 export CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES={N}\\n- [2026-05-15] GPU 碰撞：提交前 nvidia-smi 确认目标 GPU 无其他进程\\n- [2026-05-15] **同 GPU 并行 eval+training 严重降速**：cuda:1 上 eval+training 并行导致训练从 7s/step 恶化到 35-49s/step。eval 必须用独立 GPU 或等训练完成。不要因\\\"eval 几分钟就完\\\"而在同一 GPU 上并行\\n- [2026-05-15] **重复 Worker 竞态**：两个同名 Worker 相隔 30 秒启动，后者看到前者的修改误判为\\\"原有\\\"。派 Worker 前查 list_workers 确认无重复任务\\n- [2026-05-15] **Bootstrap 方法一致性**：同一论文表格中所有 pairwise CI 必须用同一方法（paired 或 unpaired）。evaluate.py 必须加 --save_predictions 保存 per-sample 结果\\n\\n## 文献定位 [2026-05-15]\\n- Wang et al. (EMNLP 2025): 原始 trace 对 SFT 帮助有限\\n- APPT (TSE 2024): 最近 competitor，BERT 非 decoder，无 trace\\n- **文献空白**：无现有工作做 compressed execution trace + SFT for binary verdict\\n- Ferrando et al. 2025: compact ≥ full scratchpad\\n- TraceCoder (ICSE 2026): 选择性 probe 优于全量 trace\\n- TraceRepair (2026): critical variable snapshots > 全量\\n- Demystifying Errors (2025): LLM 值追踪能力弱\\n\", \"total_chars\": 9547, \"truncated\": false}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {}}"

```text
1	\section{Related Work}
2	
3	\subsection{Execution Traces for Code LLMs}
4	
5	Execution traces have been increasingly explored as supplementary signals for code language models, but existing work focuses almost exclusively on generation tasks such as code repair and synthesis.
6	\cite{placeholder_WangEMNLP2025} provide the most comprehensive study to date, showing that execution trace SFT yields limited improvements for code generation across multiple settings---a finding we complement by demonstrating that traces become highly effective when the downstream task shifts from generation to classification.
7	\cite{placeholder_NiICML2024} use self-training on execution traces as chain-of-thought rationales, improving MBPP fix rates by 26.1\%, while \cite{placeholder_JungEMNLP2025} extract step-by-step traces via the Python debugger Snoop to construct CoT supervision.
8	Both treat traces as intermediate reasoning scaffolds for producing code, leaving the question of trace utility for binary judgments entirely unexamined.
9	
10	A parallel line of work investigates what trace content to retain.
11	\cite{placeholder_FerrandoArXiv2025} show that compact scratchpads recording only changed values match or exceed full-state scratchpads, providing early evidence that value-level information is largely redundant.
12	\cite{placeholder_DemystifyingArXiv2025} corroborate this from the model capability side, finding that LLMs are substantially weaker at value tracking than at structural understanding.
13	TraceCoder \cite{placeholder_TraceCoderICSE2026} demonstrates that selective variable probing outperforms full traces by 34.43\% on Pass@1, and TraceRepair \cite{placeholder_TraceRepairArXiv2026} uses critical variable snapshots to repair 392 defects.
14	ND-Slicer \cite{placeholder_YadavallyFSE2024} proposes execution-aware pretraining to learn dynamic dependencies, and \cite{placeholder_PredSlicingFSE2024} introduces assertion-point slicing conceptually close to our snapshot design.
15	\cite{placeholder_HaqueACL2025} observe that trace benefits diminish as program complexity increases, while ET-CoT \cite{placeholder_ETCoTICML2025} pushes granularity to the sub-line level on CRUXEval.
16	
17	These compression strategies all optimize traces for generation fluency.
18	Our work departs from this paradigm: for a classification objective with $O(1)$ output entropy, the optimal representation is not a compressed execution narrative but a decision-relevant event summary---a principle that yields 99.2\% verdict accuracy where full traces reach only 81.8\%.
19	
20	\subsection{Fine-Tuning LLMs for Classification}
21	
22	Adapting generative LLMs to classification raises distinct design questions around input representation and training objective.
23	\cite{placeholder_YousefiArXiv2025} compare embedding-based and instruction-based SFT for text classification, finding task-dependent trade-offs between the two paradigms.
24	FisherSFT \cite{placeholder_FisherSFTICML2025} leverages Fisher information to select maximally informative training subsets, improving sample efficiency for classification fine-tuning.
25	\cite{placeholder_BucherArXiv2024} show that fine-tuned small models consistently outperform zero-shot GPT-4 on classification benchmarks, reinforcing that SFT remains the dominant approach for binary prediction tasks.
26	
27	None of these studies consider structured program execution data as input, nor do they examine how input granularity interacts with classification accuracy.
28	Our work bridges this gap by treating the trace format itself as a first-class design variable: when the task is binary verdict prediction, the choice of trace abstraction has a larger effect on accuracy (+24.1 points) than the choice of whether to include traces at all (+6.7 points).
29	
30	\subsection{Code Correctness Prediction}
31	
32	Predicting whether code is correct constitutes the task most directly related to ours, yet prior approaches rely on static representations.
33	APPT \cite{placeholder_APPTTSE2024} combines BERT embeddings with LSTM sequence modeling for automated patch correctness classification, reaching 79.7\% accuracy---the closest prior baseline to our verdict task setting.
34	\cite{placeholder_SpiessICSE2025} show that code generation models are poorly calibrated when repurposed for correctness judgments, motivating dedicated classification architectures over repurposed generators.
35	At industry scale, \cite{placeholder_FosterFSE2025} build a mutation equivalence detector at Meta achieving 0.95 precision, demonstrating that binary code judgments can reach high reliability through domain-specific design.
36	
37	No prior code correctness predictor incorporates execution traces as input.
38	VOTA closes this gap by pairing trace abstraction with verdict-specific compression: reducing full execution traces to exception-type events raises accuracy from 75.1\% (no trace) past 81.8\% (full trace) to 99.2\%, with the exception-only variant reaching 99.83\%---indicating that for pass/fail judgments, knowing \emph{what went wrong} matters far more than knowing \emph{what happened}.
39	
```

</details>

```text
1	\section{Experiments}
2	
3	We evaluate our trace preprocessing pipeline across two research questions:
4	(1)~Do Tier~1 exception signals generalize across models of different capability?
5	(2)~What is the relative contribution of each trace component to verdict prediction accuracy?
6	All experiments use the TracePile benchmark \citep{chen2025tracepile} with $N{=}1{,}190$ test samples; accuracy is reported with Wilson 95\% confidence intervals unless stated otherwise.
7	
8	\subsection{Cross-Model Comparison}
9	\label{sec:cross_model}
10	
11	To test whether exception-based trace compression is model-agnostic, we compare Qwen-2.5-7B-Instruct and DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B-Instruct under four input conditions: \texttt{exception\_only} (Tier~1 exception events), VOTA (exception + assertion-point + loop compression), \texttt{full\_trace} (complete execution trace), and \texttt{no\_trace} (source code only).
12	
13	\begin{table}[t]
14	\centering
15	\small
16	\begin{tabular}{lccc}
17	\toprule
18	\textbf{Condition} & \textbf{Qwen-2.5-7B} & \textbf{DeepSeek-6.7B} & \textbf{Gap} \\
19	\midrule
20	exception\_only & \textbf{99.83}\% & 99.33\%$^\dag$ & 0.50pp \\
21	VOTA & 99.41\% & 84.45\% & 14.96pp \\
22	full\_trace & 81.76\% & N/A$^\ddag$ & --- \\
23	no\_trace & 75.13\% & 73.36\% & 1.77pp \\
24	\midrule
25	Majority baseline & 71.51\% & 71.51\% & --- \\
26	\bottomrule
27	\end{tabular}
28	\caption{Cross-model accuracy on TracePile ($N{=}1{,}190$). Qwen CIs: exception\_only [99.50, 100.00], VOTA [98.82, 99.83], full\_trace [79.70, 83.90], no\_trace [72.77, 77.48]; DeepSeek CIs: VOTA [82.60, 86.40], no\_trace [70.80, 75.70]. $^\dag$DeepSeek exception\_only reached 99.83\% at epoch~1 but degraded to 99.33\% at convergence (single seed, no CI). $^\ddag$DeepSeek full\_trace was invalidated due to an \texttt{evaluate.py} truncation mismatch between training and evaluation; see Limitations.}
29	\label{tab:cross_model}
30	\end{table}
31	
32	Table~\ref{tab:cross_model} reveals a striking asymmetry.
33	Under \texttt{exception\_only}, the cross-model gap is only 0.50 percentage points (pp), indicating that exception events carry a near-sufficient signal regardless of model capacity.
34	Under VOTA, the gap widens to 14.96pp: DeepSeek drops to 84.45\% while Qwen remains at 99.41\%.
35	This pattern suggests that processing mixed trace components (loop compressions, assertion points) demands model capability that the smaller DeepSeek lacks, whereas isolated exception events are interpretable by both models.
36	
37	The \texttt{no\_trace} baseline confirms that source code alone provides limited signal (75.13\% for Qwen, 73.36\% for DeepSeek), only marginally above majority-class guessing (71.51\%).
38	
39	Seed robustness for Qwen \texttt{exception\_only} was evaluated across three seeds (42, 123, 456): all three produced identical accuracy of 99.83\% with exactly two errors each ($\sigma{=}0$, $n{=}3$).
40	
41	\subsection{Component Ablation}
42	\label{sec:ablation}
43	
44	We decompose the VOTA pipeline into individual components on Qwen-2.5-7B (seed=42).
45	Table~\ref{tab:ablation} arranges conditions into a two-tier hierarchy: Tier~1 signals (\texttt{ae\_only}, \texttt{exception\_only}) that individually achieve ${\geq}$99.8\% accuracy, and Tier~2 signals (\texttt{loop\_only}) that provide moderate improvement over the no-trace baseline.
46	
47	\begin{table}[t]
48	\centering
49	\small
50	\begin{tabular}{llccc}
51	\toprule
52	\textbf{Condition} & \textbf{Components} & \textbf{Acc (\%)} & \textbf{95\% CI} & \textbf{Tier} \\
53	\midrule
54	ae\_only & assertion-point & \textbf{99.92} & [99.75, 100.00] & 1 \\
55	exception\_only & exception events & 99.83 & [99.50, 100.00] & 1 \\
56	VOTA & exc+ae+loop & 99.41 & [98.82, 99.83] & --- \\
57	VOTA\_no\_ae & exc+loop & 99.41 & [98.90, 99.80] & --- \\
58	loop\_only & loop compression & 85.63 & [83.53, 87.56] & 2 \\
59	full\_trace & complete trace & 81.76 & [79.70, 83.90] & --- \\
60	no\_trace & none & 75.13 & [72.77, 77.48] & --- \\
61	\bottomrule
62	\end{tabular}
63	\caption{Component ablation on Qwen-2.5-7B ($N{=}1{,}190$, seed=42). Tier~1 signals individually achieve near-perfect accuracy; combining them (VOTA) slightly dilutes performance.}
64	\label{tab:ablation}
65	\end{table}
66	
67	Two findings stand out.
68	First, the Tier~1 signals are individually sufficient: \texttt{ae\_only} (99.92\%) and \texttt{exception\_only} (99.83\%) are not distinguishable ($p{=}0.80$, McNemar's test), and both exceed VOTA (99.41\%, $p{=}0.01$ for \texttt{exception\_only} vs.\ VOTA).
69	This implies that combining signals in VOTA introduces minor noise rather than complementary information.
70	Second, removing assertion points from VOTA has no effect (VOTA\_no\_ae = VOTA = 99.41\%), confirming that within the VOTA mixture, exception events dominate the prediction signal.
71	
72	Tier~2 signal \texttt{loop\_only} (85.63\%) improves over \texttt{no\_trace} (75.13\%) by 10.50pp, showing that loop compression captures some execution semantics, but far less than exception events.
73	
74	\subsection{Information Density Decomposition}
75	\label{sec:density}
76	
77	The gap between \texttt{full\_trace} (81.76\%) and VOTA (99.41\%) is 17.65pp.
78	We decompose this gap into two mechanisms by introducing an intermediate condition, \texttt{full\_trace\_label\_only}, which retains the complete trace structure but replaces raw values with type labels.
79	
80	\begin{table}[t]
81	\centering
82	\small
83	\begin{tabular}{lccc}
84	\toprule
85	\textbf{Condition} & \textbf{Acc (\%)} & \textbf{$\Delta$ vs.\ full\_trace} & \textbf{Gap explained} \\
86	\midrule
87	full\_trace & 81.76 & --- & --- \\
88	label\_only & 86.22 & +4.46pp & 26\% \\
89	VOTA & 99.41 & +17.65pp & 100\% \\
90	\bottomrule
91	\end{tabular}
92	\caption{Information density decomposition. The full\_trace$\to$VOTA gap decomposes into gradient dilution (26\%, from removing low-density tokens) and information overload (74\%, from removing irrelevant trace lines).}
93	\label{tab:density}
94	\end{table}
95	
96	As shown in Table~\ref{tab:density}, the decomposition attributes 26\% of the gap (+4.46pp) to \emph{gradient dilution}---the presence of raw variable values that expand token count without adding predictive signal---and 74\% (+13.19pp) to \emph{information overload}---the inclusion of trace lines that are irrelevant to the fault.
97	This two-factor decomposition is consistent across models: the VOTA cross-model gap (14.96pp, Table~\ref{tab:cross_model}) tracks model capability, suggesting that weaker models are disproportionately affected by information overload.
98	
99	\subsection{Calibration Analysis}
100	\label{sec:calibration}
101	
102	Beyond accuracy, we evaluate whether trace conditions affect prediction calibration.
103	Figure~\ref{fig:calibration} shows reliability diagrams for four conditions on Qwen-2.5-7B.
104	
105	\begin{table}[t]
106	\centering
107	\small
108	\begin{tabular}{lcc}
109	\toprule
110	\textbf{Condition} & \textbf{ECE $\downarrow$} & \textbf{Brier $\downarrow$} \\
111	\midrule
112	ae\_only & \textbf{0.0004} & \textbf{0.001} \\
113	exception\_only & 0.003 & 0.001 \\
114	VOTA & 0.005 & 0.005 \\
115	full\_trace & 0.096 & 0.144 \\
116	no\_trace & 0.172 & 0.197 \\
117	\bottomrule
118	\end{tabular}
119	\caption{Calibration metrics on Qwen-2.5-7B ($N{=}1{,}190$, seed=42). ECE = expected calibration error; Brier = Brier score. Lower is better for both.}
120	\label{tab:calibration}
121	\end{table}
122	
123	Table~\ref{tab:calibration} shows that calibration quality mirrors the Tier~1/2 hierarchy.
124	Tier~1 conditions (\texttt{ae\_only}: ECE=0.0004; \texttt{exception\_only}: ECE=0.003) produce near-perfectly calibrated predictions, while \texttt{full\_trace} (ECE=0.096) and \texttt{no\_trace} (ECE=0.172) exhibit substantial miscalibration.
125	VOTA (ECE=0.005) is well-calibrated but slightly worse than either Tier~1 component alone, reinforcing the pattern that combining trace signals dilutes rather than improves the prediction signal.
126	
127	The Brier scores tell a consistent story: \texttt{exception\_only} (0.001) and \texttt{ae\_only} (0.001) achieve near-zero scores, while \texttt{no\_trace} (0.197) approaches the theoretical maximum for a binary classifier at this class ratio.
128

{
  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_4/projects/trace-verdict-study/artifacts/_project/paper/section3_experimental_setup.tex",
  "content": "\\section{Experimental Setup}\n\\label{sec:setup}\n\n\\subsection{Task Definition}\n\\label{sec:task}\n\nWe study \\emph{verdict prediction}: given a code snippet $c$, a test input $i$, and an expected output $o_{\\text{exp}}$, predict whether executing $c$ on $i$ produces $o_{\\text{exp}}$.\nLet $\\mathbf{x} = (c, i, o_{\\text{exp}})$ denote the static context and $t$ the full execution trace obtained by running $c(i)$.\nEach experimental condition defines a trace preprocessing function $f$ such that the model receives $(\\mathbf{x}, f(t))$ and generates a verdict $\\hat{y} \\in \\{\\text{pass}, \\text{fail}\\}$.\n\nWe formulate verdict prediction as supervised fine-tuning (SFT) in an instruction-tuned chat format.\nThe model generates a single token---\\texttt{pass} or \\texttt{fail}---as its response, which constitutes a single token in both the Qwen and DeepSeek tokenizers used in our experiments.\nThis setup differs fundamentally from code generation: the output space is $O(1)$, so all task-relevant information must be extracted from the input representation.\nThe trace preprocessing function $f$ is therefore the primary design variable.\n\n\\subsection{Dataset}\n\\label{sec:dataset}\n\nWe use the verdict prediction dataset constructed by \\cite{placeholder_WangEMNLP2025} from CRUXEval \\citep{placeholder_GuICML2024}, a benchmark of 800 standalone Python functions designed for execution reasoning evaluation.\n\\cite{placeholder_WangEMNLP2025} paired each function with multiple test cases and collected line-by-line execution traces via a Python tracer, recording every executed line, variable assignment, function call, and exception event.\nThis produced 5{,}910 labeled examples, each containing the source code, test input, expected output, ground-truth verdict, and full execution trace.\n\nWe split the data into 4{,}720 training and 1{,}190 test examples.\nThe test set is pass-dominant: 71.51\\% of samples have verdict \\emph{pass}, establishing a majority-class baseline of 71.51\\% accuracy.\nThis class imbalance motivates our use of per-class precision, recall, and F1 alongside overall accuracy.\n\n\\subsection{Trace Representations}\n\\label{sec:vota}\n\n\\paragraph{Full trace.}\nThe raw execution trace records every line execution, variable state, and function call in sequential order.\nFor models with limited context, traces are tokenized and truncated to fit a 2{,}048-token window.\nWe apply chat-template-aware truncation that reserves token budget for the verdict label at the sequence end, preventing the label from being right-truncated during tokenization---a failure mode that causes complete training collapse (Appendix~\\ref{app:truncation}).\n\n\\paragraph{VOTA.}\nVariable-Outcome Trace Abstraction (VOTA) extracts three diagnostic components from the full trace:\n\n\\begin{enumerate}[itemsep=2pt,parsep=0pt,topsep=4pt]\n\\item \\textbf{Exception summary.}\nStructured error events in the format \\texttt{[ERROR] type: \\textit{ExceptionType} message: \\textit{msg}}.\nPresent only when execution raises an exception; absent for successful runs.\n\n\\item \\textbf{Assertion point.}\nA snapshot of the actual and expected output values: \\texttt{[ASSERT] expected: \\textit{X} actual: \\textit{Y}}.\nCaptures the diagnostic moment where the execution outcome can be compared against the specification.\n\n\\item \\textbf{Loop compression.}\nA behavioral summary replacing repetitive per-iteration trace lines with iteration count and variable change trends (e.g., \\texttt{3 iterations, result grows [2] $\\to$ [2,4] $\\to$ [2,4,6]}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n\nVOTA is not a lossy compression of the full trace.\nIt performs \\emph{targeted extraction}: each component isolates a specific diagnostic signal while discarding all intermediate execution state.\nPrior trace compression work optimizes for generation fluency by retaining a narrative of execution \\citep{placeholder_FerrandoArXiv2025,placeholder_TraceCoderICSE2026}; VOTA instead selects the minimal set of events sufficient for a binary judgment.\nFigure~\\ref{fig:vota_example} illustrates the transformation.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\fbox{\\parbox{0.93\\columnwidth}{%\n\\small\n\\textbf{Full trace} (excerpt, 847 tokens):\\\\[2pt]\n\\texttt{L2: x = [1, 2, 3]}\\\\\n\\texttt{L3: result = []}\\\\\n\\texttt{L4: for item in x: ~~\\# iter 1}\\\\\n\\texttt{L5: ~~result.append(item * 2)}\\\\\n\\texttt{L4: for item in x: ~~\\# iter 2}\\\\\n\\texttt{L5: ~~result.append(item * 2)}\\\\\n\\texttt{~~\\ldots~(14 more lines)}\\\\[6pt]\n\\textbf{VOTA} (38 tokens):\\\\[2pt]\n\\texttt{[LOOP] L4--5: 3 iters, result}\\\\\n\\texttt{~~grows [2] $\\to$ [2,4] $\\to$ [2,4,6]}\\\\\n\\texttt{[ASSERT] expected: [2,4,6]}\\\\\n\\texttt{~~~~~~~~ actual: [2,4,6]}\\\\\n\\texttt{[No exception]}}}\n\\caption{Full trace to VOTA transformation. VOTA reduces token count by ${\\sim}$20$\\times$ while retaining only signals relevant to the pass/fail verdict.}\n\\label{fig:vota_example}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\subsection{Experimental Conditions}\n\\label{sec:conditions}\n\n\\paragraph{Conditions.}\nWe evaluate seven trace conditions on Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct \\citep{placeholder_Qwen25Coder} and four on DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B-Instruct \\citep{placeholder_DeepSeekCoder}.\nTable~\\ref{tab:conditions} lists all conditions.\nThe first four conditions are shared across both models; the remaining three---component-level ablations and a loss ablation---are evaluated on Qwen only.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lp{4.9cm}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Condition} & \\textbf{Input to model} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\texttt{no\\_trace} & $\\mathbf{x}$ only \\\\\n\\texttt{full\\_trace} & $\\mathbf{x} + t$, truncated to 2048 tokens \\\\\n\\texttt{VOTA} & $\\mathbf{x} + \\text{VOTA}(t)$ \\\\\n\\texttt{exception\\_only} & $\\mathbf{x}$ + exception component \\\\\n\\texttt{ae\\_only}$^*$ & $\\mathbf{x}$ + assertion-point component \\\\\n\\texttt{loop\\_only}$^*$ & $\\mathbf{x}$ + loop compression component \\\\\n\\texttt{label\\_only\\_loss}$^{*\\dag}$ & $\\mathbf{x} + t$; loss on verdict token only \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\caption{Experimental conditions. The first four are evaluated on both Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B and DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B; $^*$conditions are Qwen-only. $^\\dag$\\texttt{label\\_only\\_loss} uses the same input as \\texttt{full\\_trace} but restricts the training loss to the verdict token, isolating gradient dilution from information overload.}\n\\label{tab:conditions}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Training.}\nAll conditions use identical hyperparameters.\nWe fine-tune with LoRA \\citep{placeholder_HuLoRA2022}: rank 16, $\\alpha{=}32$, applied to all linear layers.\nTraining runs for 3 epochs with learning rate $2{\\times}10^{-4}$, per-device batch size 4, and gradient accumulation over 8 steps (effective batch size 32).\nMaximum sequence length is 2{,}048 tokens.\nGradient checkpointing is enabled for all runs.\nThe primary seed is 42; we run two additional seeds (123, 456) for \\texttt{exception\\_only} to assess robustness of the headline result.\n\nAll experiments run on 2$\\times$ NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 GPUs (96\\,GB each) with CUDA 12.8.\nEach single-GPU training run completes in approximately 2 hours.\n\n\\paragraph{Evaluation.}\nWe report accuracy with bootstrap 95\\% confidence intervals ($B{=}10{,}000$).\nPairwise comparisons use paired bootstrap tests on per-sample predictions.\nWe additionally report per-class precision, recall, and F1, as well as expected calibration error \\citep[ECE;][]{placeholder_NaeiniICML2015} and Brier score to assess prediction confidence.\n"
}

VOTA reduces token count by ${\\sim}$20$\\times$ while retaining only signals relevant to the pass/fail verdict.}\n\\label{fig:vota_example}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\subsection{Experimental Conditions}\n\\label{sec:conditions}\n\n\\paragraph{Conditions.}\nWe evaluate seven trace conditions on Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct \\citep{placeholder_Qwen25Coder} and four on DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B-Instruct \\citep{placeholder_DeepSeekCoder}.\nTable~\\ref{tab:conditions} lists all conditions.\nThe first four conditions are shared across both models; the remaining three---component-level ablations and a loss ablation---are evaluated on Qwen only.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lp{4.9cm}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Condition} & \\textbf{Input to model} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\texttt{no\\_trace} & $\\mathbf{x}$ only \\\\\n\\texttt{full\\_trace} & $\\mathbf{x} + t$, truncated to 2048 tokens \\\\\n\\texttt{VOTA} & $\\mathbf{x} + \\text{VOTA}(t)$ \\\\\n\\texttt{exception\\_only} & $\\mathbf{x}$ + exception component \\\\\n\\texttt{ae\\_only}$^*$ & $\\mathbf{x}$ + assertion-point component \\\\\n\\texttt{loop\\_only}$^*$ & $\\mathbf{x}$ + loop compression component \\\\\n\\texttt{label\\_only\\_loss}$^{*\\dag}$ & $\\mathbf{x} + t$; loss on verdict token only \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\caption{Experimental conditions. The first four are evaluated on both Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B and DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B; $^*$conditions are Qwen-only. $^\\dag$\\texttt{label\\_only\\_loss} uses the same input as \\texttt{full\\_trace} but restricts the training loss to the verdict token, isolating gradient dilution from information overload.}\n\\label{tab:conditions}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Training.}\nAll conditions use identical hyperparameters.\nWe fine-tune with LoRA \\citep{placeholder_HuLoRA2022}: rank 16, $\\alpha{=}32$, applied to all linear layers.\nTraining runs for 3 epochs with learning rate $2{\\times}10^{-4}$, per-device batch size 4, and gradient accumulation over 8 steps (effective batch size 32).\nMaximum sequence length is 2{,}048 tokens.\nGradient checkpointing is enabled for all runs.\nThe primary seed is 42; we run two additional seeds (123, 456) for \\texttt{exception\\_only} to assess robustness of the headline result.\n\nAll experiments run on 2$\\times$ NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 GPUs (96\\,GB each) with CUDA 12.8.\nEach single-GPU training run completes in approximately 2 hours.\n\n\\paragraph{Evaluation.}\nWe report accuracy with bootstrap 95\\% confidence intervals ($B{=}10{,}000$).\nPairwise comparisons use paired bootstrap tests on per-sample predictions.\nWe additionally report per-class precision, recall, and F1, as well as expected calibration error \\citep[ECE;][]{placeholder_NaeiniICML2015} and Brier score to assess prediction confidence.\n",
